========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, hello doctor. I guess what brings me in today is... I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. I know I've been doing farm work for a long time, but the cracks, the spots, the sagging skin—it's just really getting to me. 

Whenever I have to go into town and deal with a banker or some county person, I feel like they're just staring right at it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a man who's worked outdoors, but... I don't know. I just can't stop focusing on it, and it's making things hard.

Yeah, I really do. Like I was just saying, I just can't seem to get past it. It's on my mind pretty much all day.

I just can't let people see me like that. And like I mentioned, if I have to go into town, it's all I can focus on. I just keep picturing those bankers or county folks staring at me, judging how worn out I look. My wife keeps telling me I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a farmer, but... yeah, it's definitely something I'm thinking about most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about the appearance of your face and hands interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean... I've actually missed calls with the bankers, and I've put off some important farm decisions lately. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to actually sit down in a room with people. \n\nAnd on top of the regular farm work, we have a retreat and do some craft sales, and I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't let people see me like that. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands that you're concerned about, or is it your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying before, it's really just those specific things. I mean, my overall appearance... I don't really worry about the rest of my body. \n\nIt's just... I really can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin—that's exactly what I feel like every banker or county person is staring at when I talk to them. Even though my wife keeps telling me I just look like a normal guy who's worked outdoors his whole life, those specific areas are what I'm completely hung up on. So, yeah, it's not everything, just the face and the hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, to me, they really do. When I look in the mirror or look down at my hands, I see exactly how bad they are. \n\nI know my wife keeps saying I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a regular guy who works outside, so... I don't know, maybe other people don't see it quite as harsh as I do. But yeah, when I'm looking at myself, I absolutely think they look as bad as I feel they do. It's hard to believe anything else when it's right there in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands don't look as bad as you think they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess there's a possibility. I mean, my wife is pretty honest with me, and she swears I'm blowing it out of proportion. So maybe logically I know that's possible. But it sure doesn't feel that way when I look at them. I just can't shake the feeling that they look awful, even if part of me knows I might be wrong.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the cracks, spots, or sagging skin that you think makes them particularly unattractive or problematic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if there's one specific thing... it's just the whole combination of it. It just makes me look so old and weathered, you know? Like I haven't taken care of myself or something. I guess I just feel like it makes me look... I don't know, worn out. Like I'm past my prime and everyone can see the toll the work has taken on me. It's just hard to look at.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know I've been doing farm work for a long time, but the cracks, the spots, the sagging skin—it's just really getting to me. \n\nWhenever I have to go into town and deal with a banker or some county person, I feel like they're just staring right at it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a man who's worked outdoors, but... I don't know. I just can't stop focusing on it, and it's making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the appearance of your face and hands most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, hello doctor. I guess what brings me in today is... I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. I know I've been doing farm work for a long time, but the cracks, the spots, the sagging skin—it's just really getting to me. \n\nWhenever I have to go into town and deal with a banker or some county person, I feel like they're just staring right at it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a man who's worked outdoors, but... I don't know. I just can't stop focusing on it, and it's making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the appearance of your face and hands most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Like I was just saying, I just can't seem to get past it. It's on my mind pretty much all day. \n\nI'll just be going about my day, and I keep focusing on the cracks and the spots, or thinking about how my face sags. And like I mentioned, if I have to go into town, it's all I can focus on. I just keep picturing those bankers or county folks staring at me, judging how worn out I look. My wife keeps telling me I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a farmer, but... yeah, it's definitely something I'm thinking about most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about the appearance of your face and hands interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean... I've actually missed calls with the bankers, and I've put off some important farm decisions lately. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to actually sit down in a room with people. \n\nAnd on top of the regular farm work, we have a retreat and do some craft sales, and I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't let people see me like that. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands that you're concerned about, or is it your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying before, it's really just those specific things. I mean, my overall appearance... I don't really worry about the rest of my body. \n\nIt's just... I really can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin—that's exactly what I feel like every banker or county person is staring at when I talk to them. Even though my wife keeps telling me I just look like a normal guy who's worked outdoors his whole life, those specific areas are what I'm completely hung up on. So, yeah, it's not everything, just the face and the hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, to me, they really do. When I look in the mirror or look down at my hands, I see exactly how bad they are. \n\nI know my wife keeps saying I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a regular guy who works outside, so... I don't know, maybe other people don't see it quite as harsh as I do. But yeah, when I'm looking at myself, I absolutely think they look as bad as I feel they do. It's hard to believe anything else when it's right there in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands don't look as bad as you think they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess there's a possibility. I mean, my wife is pretty honest with me, and she swears I'm blowing it out of proportion. So maybe logically I know that's possible. But it sure doesn't feel that way when I look at them. I just can't shake the feeling that they look awful, even if part of me knows I might be wrong.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the cracks, spots, or sagging skin that you think makes them particularly unattractive or problematic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if there's one specific thing... it's just the whole combination of it. It just makes me look so old and weathered, you know? Like I haven't taken care of myself or something. I guess I just feel like it makes me look... I don't know, worn out. Like I'm past my prime and everyone can see the toll the work has taken on me. It's just hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time where someone's compliment or reassurance about your appearance made you feel better about your face and hands for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my wife tries, like I mentioned before. She'll tell me I look fine, or that the spots and lines aren't a big deal. But... it doesn't really change anything for me. \n\nI might appreciate her trying to be nice, but as soon as I look down at my hands again or catch my face in the mirror, I'm right back to seeing it all. It just doesn't sink in. So, no, I wouldn't say reassurance really helps or makes me feel better about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could change over time, or do you feel they will always be a problem for you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... I'd like to think it could get better, maybe if I stopped working out in the sun so much, or... I don't know. But right now, it feels like it's just going to keep getting worse. The older I get, the more the skin sags and the more spots show up. So, to be honest, I kind of feel like this is just how it's going to be from here on out. It's hard to imagine not being bothered by it.\n\nDoctor: If you were to receive a compliment from someone you really respect about your appearance, do you think it would change how you feel about your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I really don't think it would. I mean... even if it was someone I had a lot of respect for, I'd probably just think they were being polite. Or, you know, maybe I'd feel okay for a minute or two, but as soon as I saw my hands again, or caught my reflection... I'd just see the exact same sagging skin and spots all over again. \n\nWhat someone else says doesn't change what's actually right there in front of me. So, no, I don't think a compliment would really fix how I see it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's on my mind pretty much all day. \n\nI'll just be going about my day, and I keep focusing on the cracks and the spots, or thinking about how my face sags. And like I mentioned, if I have to go into town, it's all I can focus on. I just keep picturing those bankers or county folks staring at me, judging how worn out I look. My wife keeps telling me I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a farmer, but... yeah, it's definitely something I'm thinking about most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about the appearance of your face and hands interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean... I've actually missed calls with the bankers, and I've put off some important farm decisions lately. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to actually sit down in a room with people. \n\nAnd on top of the regular farm work, we have a retreat and do some craft sales, and I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't let people see me like that. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands that you're concerned about, or is it your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying before, it's really just those specific things. I mean, my overall appearance... I don't really worry about the rest of my body. \n\nIt's just... I really can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin—that's exactly what I feel like every banker or county person is staring at when I talk to them. Even though my wife keeps telling me I just look like a normal guy who's worked outdoors his whole life, those specific areas are what I'm completely hung up on. So, yeah, it's not everything, just the face and the hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, to me, they really do. When I look in the mirror or look down at my hands, I see exactly how bad they are. \n\nI know my wife keeps saying I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a regular guy who works outside, so... I don't know, maybe other people don't see it quite as harsh as I do. But yeah, when I'm looking at myself, I absolutely think they look as bad as I feel they do. It's hard to believe anything else when it's right there in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands don't look as bad as you think they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess there's a possibility. I mean, my wife is pretty honest with me, and she swears I'm blowing it out of proportion. So maybe logically I know that's possible. But it sure doesn't feel that way when I look at them. I just can't shake the feeling that they look awful, even if part of me knows I might be wrong.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the cracks, spots, or sagging skin that you think makes them particularly unattractive or problematic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if there's one specific thing... it's just the whole combination of it. It just makes me look so old and weathered, you know? Like I haven't taken care of myself or something. I guess I just feel like it makes me look... I don't know, worn out. Like I'm past my prime and everyone can see the toll the work has taken on me. It's just hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time where someone's compliment or reassurance about your appearance made you feel better about your face and hands for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my wife tries, like I mentioned before. She'll tell me I look fine, or that the spots and lines aren't a big deal. But... it doesn't really change anything for me. \n\nI might appreciate her trying to be nice, but as soon as I look down at my hands again or catch my face in the mirror, I'm right back to seeing it all. It just doesn't sink in. So, no, I wouldn't say reassurance really helps or makes me feel better about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could change over time, or do you feel they will always be a problem for you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... I'd like to think it could get better, maybe if I stopped working out in the sun so much, or... I don't know. But right now, it feels like it's just going to keep getting worse. The older I get, the more the skin sags and the more spots show up. So, to be honest, I kind of feel like this is just how it's going to be from here on out. It's hard to imagine not being bothered by it.\n\nDoctor: If you were to receive a compliment from someone you really respect about your appearance, do you think it would change how you feel about your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I really don't think it would. I mean... even if it was someone I had a lot of respect for, I'd probably just think they were being polite. Or, you know, maybe I'd feel okay for a minute or two, but as soon as I saw my hands again, or caught my reflection... I'd just see the exact same sagging skin and spots all over again. \n\nWhat someone else says doesn't change what's actually right there in front of me. So, no, I don't think a compliment would really fix how I see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could lessen if you were able to change your appearance in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about changing my appearance, like with surgery or anything like that. I guess I just don't know if it would actually help, or if I'd just find something else wrong with how it turned out. So, I don't really have a good answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular aspect of your face or hands that you think is the most unattractive or bothersome to you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's really the whole rough and worn look of them. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin. It's... it's just what brought me in here in the first place. I can't get past how bad it looks. Whenever I have to go into town, I feel like every banker or county clerk is just staring right at those cracks and spots. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't see it that way. I just see how ruined my skin looks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It just makes me look so old and weathered, you know? Like I haven't taken care of myself or something. I guess I just feel like it makes me look... I don't know, worn out. Like I'm past my prime and everyone can see the toll the work has taken on me. It's just hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time where someone's compliment or reassurance about your appearance made you feel better about your face and hands for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my wife tries, like I mentioned before. She'll tell me I look fine, or that the spots and lines aren't a big deal. But... it doesn't really change anything for me. \n\nI might appreciate her trying to be nice, but as soon as I look down at my hands again or catch my face in the mirror, I'm right back to seeing it all. It just doesn't sink in. So, no, I wouldn't say reassurance really helps or makes me feel better about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could change over time, or do you feel they will always be a problem for you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... I'd like to think it could get better, maybe if I stopped working out in the sun so much, or... I don't know. But right now, it feels like it's just going to keep getting worse. The older I get, the more the skin sags and the more spots show up. So, to be honest, I kind of feel like this is just how it's going to be from here on out. It's hard to imagine not being bothered by it.\n\nDoctor: If you were to receive a compliment from someone you really respect about your appearance, do you think it would change how you feel about your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I really don't think it would. I mean... even if it was someone I had a lot of respect for, I'd probably just think they were being polite. Or, you know, maybe I'd feel okay for a minute or two, but as soon as I saw my hands again, or caught my reflection... I'd just see the exact same sagging skin and spots all over again. \n\nWhat someone else says doesn't change what's actually right there in front of me. So, no, I don't think a compliment would really fix how I see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could lessen if you were able to change your appearance in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about changing my appearance, like with surgery or anything like that. I guess I just don't know if it would actually help, or if I'd just find something else wrong with how it turned out. So, I don't really have a good answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular aspect of your face or hands that you think is the most unattractive or bothersome to you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's really the whole rough and worn look of them. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin. It's... it's just what brought me in here in the first place. I can't get past how bad it looks. Whenever I have to go into town, I feel like every banker or county clerk is just staring right at those cracks and spots. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't see it that way. I just see how ruined my skin looks.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one specific flaw in your appearance that you are most concerned about?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's just one specific thing, no. Like I was saying, it's the whole combination... the sagging, the spots, the cracks on my hands and face. It's not like there's one single spot or one deep wrinkle that bothers me more than the rest. It's just the overall way my skin looks entirely. It all just looks worn out and ruined to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, hello doctor. I guess what brings me in today is... I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. I know I've been doing farm work for a long time, but the cracks, the spots, the sagging skin—it's just really getting to me. \n\nWhenever I have to go into town and deal with a banker or some county person, I feel like they're just staring right at it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a man who's worked outdoors, but... I don't know. I just can't stop focusing on it, and it's making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the appearance of your face and hands most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Like I was just saying, I just can't seem to get past it. And like I mentioned, if I have to go into town, it's all I can focus on. I just keep picturing those bankers or county folks staring at me, judging how worn out I look. My wife keeps telling me I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a farmer, but... yeah, it's definitely something I'm thinking about most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about the appearance of your face and hands interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean... I've actually missed calls with the bankers, and I've put off some important farm decisions lately. I just can't let people see me like that. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands that you're concerned about, or is it your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying before, it's really just those specific things. I mean, my overall appearance... I don't really worry about the rest of my body. \n\nIt's just... I really can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin—that's exactly what I feel like every banker or county person is staring at when I talk to them. Even though my wife keeps telling me I just look like a normal guy who's worked outdoors his whole life, those specific areas are what I'm completely hung up on. So, yeah, it's not everything, just the face and the hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, to me, they really do. When I look in the mirror or look down at my hands, I see exactly how bad they are. \n\nI know my wife keeps saying I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a regular guy who works outside, so... I don't know, maybe other people don't see it quite as harsh as I do. But yeah, when I'm looking at myself, I absolutely think they look as bad as I feel they do. It's hard to believe anything else when it's right there in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands don't look as bad as you think they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess there's a possibility. I mean, my wife is pretty honest with me, and she swears I'm blowing it out of proportion. So maybe logically I know that's possible. But it sure doesn't feel that way when I look at them. I just can't shake the feeling that they look awful, even if part of me knows I might be wrong.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the cracks, spots, or sagging skin that you think makes them particularly unattractive or problematic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if there's one specific thing... it's just the whole combination of it. It's just the overall way my skin looks entirely. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, hello doctor. I guess what brings me in today is... I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. I know I've been doing farm work for a long time, but the cracks, the spots, the sagging skin—it's just really getting to me. \n\nWhenever I have to go into town and deal with a banker or some county person, I feel like they're just staring right at it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a man who's worked outdoors, but... I don't know. I just can't stop focusing on it, and it's making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the appearance of your face and hands most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Like I was just saying, I just can't seem to get past it. It's on my mind pretty much all day. \n\nI'll just be going about my day, and I keep focusing on the cracks and the spots, or thinking about how my face sags. And like I mentioned, if I have to go into town, it's all I can focus on. I just keep picturing those bankers or county folks staring at me, judging how worn out I look. My wife keeps telling me I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a farmer, but... yeah, it's definitely something I'm thinking about most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about the appearance of your face and hands interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean... I've actually missed calls with the bankers, and I've put off some important farm decisions lately. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to actually sit down in a room with people. \n\nAnd on top of the regular farm work, we have a retreat and do some craft sales, and I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't let people see me like that. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands that you're concerned about, or is it your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying before, it's really just those specific things. I mean, my overall appearance... I don't really worry about the rest of my body. \n\nIt's just... I really can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin—that's exactly what I feel like every banker or county person is staring at when I talk to them. Even though my wife keeps telling me I just look like a normal guy who's worked outdoors his whole life, those specific areas are what I'm completely hung up on. So, yeah, it's not everything, just the face and the hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, to me, they really do. When I look in the mirror or look down at my hands, I see exactly how bad they are. \n\nI know my wife keeps saying I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a regular guy who works outside, so... I don't know, maybe other people don't see it quite as harsh as I do. But yeah, when I'm looking at myself, I absolutely think they look as bad as I feel they do. It's hard to believe anything else when it's right there in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands don't look as bad as you think they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess there's a possibility. I mean, my wife is pretty honest with me, and she swears I'm blowing it out of proportion. So maybe logically I know that's possible. But it sure doesn't feel that way when I look at them. I just can't shake the feeling that they look awful, even if part of me knows I might be wrong.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the cracks, spots, or sagging skin that you think makes them particularly unattractive or problematic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if there's one specific thing... it's just the whole combination of it. It just makes me look so old and weathered, you know? Like I haven't taken care of myself or something. I guess I just feel like it makes me look... I don't know, worn out. Like I'm past my prime and everyone can see the toll the work has taken on me. It's just hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time where someone's compliment or reassurance about your appearance made you feel better about your face and hands for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my wife tries, like I mentioned before. She'll tell me I look fine, or that the spots and lines aren't a big deal. But... it doesn't really change anything for me. \n\nI might appreciate her trying to be nice, but as soon as I look down at my hands again or catch my face in the mirror, I'm right back to seeing it all. It just doesn't sink in. So, no, I wouldn't say reassurance really helps or makes me feel better about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could change over time, or do you feel they will always be a problem for you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... I'd like to think it could get better, maybe if I stopped working out in the sun so much, or... I don't know. But right now, it feels like it's just going to keep getting worse. The older I get, the more the skin sags and the more spots show up. So, to be honest, I kind of feel like this is just how it's going to be from here on out. It's hard to imagine not being bothered by it.\n\nDoctor: If you were to receive a compliment from someone you really respect about your appearance, do you think it would change how you feel about your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I really don't think it would. I mean... even if it was someone I had a lot of respect for, I'd probably just think they were being polite. Or, you know, maybe I'd feel okay for a minute or two, but as soon as I saw my hands again, or caught my reflection... I'd just see the exact same sagging skin and spots all over again. \n\nWhat someone else says doesn't change what's actually right there in front of me. So, no, I don't think a compliment would really fix how I see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could lessen if you were able to change your appearance in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about changing my appearance, like with surgery or anything like that. I guess I just don't know if it would actually help, or if I'd just find something else wrong with how it turned out. So, I don't really have a good answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular aspect of your face or hands that you think is the most unattractive or bothersome to you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's really the whole rough and worn look of them. Yeah, I do. I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look, and I feel like everybody else sees it just as clear. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, hello doctor. I guess what brings me in today is... I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. I know I've been doing farm work for a long time, but the cracks, the spots, the sagging skin—it's just really getting to me. \n\nWhenever I have to go into town and deal with a banker or some county person, I feel like they're just staring right at it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a man who's worked outdoors, but... I don't know. I just can't stop focusing on it, and it's making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the appearance of your face and hands most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Like I was just saying, I just can't seem to get past it. It's on my mind pretty much all day. \n\nI'll just be going about my day, and I keep focusing on the cracks and the spots, or thinking about how my face sags. And like I mentioned, if I have to go into town, it's all I can focus on. I just keep picturing those bankers or county folks staring at me, judging how worn out I look. My wife keeps telling me I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a farmer, but... yeah, it's definitely something I'm thinking about most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about the appearance of your face and hands interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean... I've actually missed calls with the bankers, and I've put off some important farm decisions lately. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to actually sit down in a room with people. \n\nAnd on top of the regular farm work, we have a retreat and do some craft sales, and I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't let people see me like that. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands that you're concerned about, or is it your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying before, it's really just those specific things. I mean, my overall appearance... I don't really worry about the rest of my body. \n\nIt's just... I really can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin—that's exactly what I feel like every banker or county person is staring at when I talk to them. Even though my wife keeps telling me I just look like a normal guy who's worked outdoors his whole life, those specific areas are what I'm completely hung up on. So, yeah, it's not everything, just the face and the hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, to me, they really do. When I look in the mirror or look down at my hands, I see exactly how bad they are. \n\nI know my wife keeps saying I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a regular guy who works outside, so... I don't know, maybe other people don't see it quite as harsh as I do. But yeah, when I'm looking at myself, I absolutely think they look as bad as I feel they do. It's hard to believe anything else when it's right there in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands don't look as bad as you think they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess there's a possibility. I mean, my wife is pretty honest with me, and she swears I'm blowing it out of proportion. So maybe logically I know that's possible. But it sure doesn't feel that way when I look at them. I just can't shake the feeling that they look awful, even if part of me knows I might be wrong.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the cracks, spots, or sagging skin that you think makes them particularly unattractive or problematic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if there's one specific thing... it's just the whole combination of it. It just makes me look so old and weathered, you know? Like I haven't taken care of myself or something. I guess I just feel like it makes me look... I don't know, worn out. Like I'm past my prime and everyone can see the toll the work has taken on me. It's just hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time where someone's compliment or reassurance about your appearance made you feel better about your face and hands for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my wife tries, like I mentioned before. She'll tell me I look fine, or that the spots and lines aren't a big deal. But... it doesn't really change anything for me. \n\nI might appreciate her trying to be nice, but as soon as I look down at my hands again or catch my face in the mirror, I'm right back to seeing it all. It just doesn't sink in. So, no, I wouldn't say reassurance really helps or makes me feel better about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could change over time, or do you feel they will always be a problem for you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... I'd like to think it could get better, maybe if I stopped working out in the sun so much, or... I don't know. But right now, it feels like it's just going to keep getting worse. The older I get, the more the skin sags and the more spots show up. So, to be honest, I kind of feel like this is just how it's going to be from here on out. It's hard to imagine not being bothered by it.\n\nDoctor: If you were to receive a compliment from someone you really respect about your appearance, do you think it would change how you feel about your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I really don't think it would. I mean... even if it was someone I had a lot of respect for, I'd probably just think they were being polite. Or, you know, maybe I'd feel okay for a minute or two, but as soon as I saw my hands again, or caught my reflection... I'd just see the exact same sagging skin and spots all over again. \n\nWhat someone else says doesn't change what's actually right there in front of me. So, no, I don't think a compliment would really fix how I see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could lessen if you were able to change your appearance in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about changing my appearance, like with surgery or anything like that. I guess I just don't know if it would actually help, or if I'd just find something else wrong with how it turned out. So, I don't really have a good answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular aspect of your face or hands that you think is the most unattractive or bothersome to you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's really the whole rough and worn look of them. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin. It's... it's just what brought me in here in the first place. I can't get past how bad it looks. Whenever I have to go into town, I feel like every banker or county clerk is just staring right at those cracks and spots. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't see it that way. I just see how ruined my skin looks.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one specific flaw in your appearance that you are most concerned about?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's just one specific thing, no. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, hello doctor. I guess what brings me in today is... I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. I know I've been doing farm work for a long time, but the cracks, the spots, the sagging skin—it's just really getting to me. \n\nWhenever I have to go into town and deal with a banker or some county person, I feel like they're just staring right at it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a man who's worked outdoors, but... I don't know. I just can't stop focusing on it, and it's making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the appearance of your face and hands most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Like I was just saying, I just can't seem to get past it. It's on my mind pretty much all day. \n\nI'll just be going about my day, and I keep focusing on the cracks and the spots, or thinking about how my face sags. And like I mentioned, if I have to go into town, it's all I can focus on. I just keep picturing those bankers or county folks staring at me, judging how worn out I look. My wife keeps telling me I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a farmer, but... yeah, it's definitely something I'm thinking about most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about the appearance of your face and hands interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean... I've actually missed calls with the bankers, and I've put off some important farm decisions lately. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to actually sit down in a room with people. \n\nAnd on top of the regular farm work, we have a retreat and do some craft sales, and I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't let people see me like that. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands that you're concerned about, or is it your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying before, it's really just those specific things. I mean, my overall appearance... I don't really worry about the rest of my body. \n\nIt's just... I really can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin—that's exactly what I feel like every banker or county person is staring at when I talk to them. Even though my wife keeps telling me I just look like a normal guy who's worked outdoors his whole life, those specific areas are what I'm completely hung up on. So, yeah, it's not everything, just the face and the hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, to me, they really do. When I look in the mirror or look down at my hands, I see exactly how bad they are. \n\nI know my wife keeps saying I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a regular guy who works outside, so... I don't know, maybe other people don't see it quite as harsh as I do. But yeah, when I'm looking at myself, I absolutely think they look as bad as I feel they do. It's hard to believe anything else when it's right there in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands don't look as bad as you think they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess there's a possibility. I mean, my wife is pretty honest with me, and she swears I'm blowing it out of proportion. So maybe logically I know that's possible. But it sure doesn't feel that way when I look at them. I just can't shake the feeling that they look awful, even if part of me knows I might be wrong.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the cracks, spots, or sagging skin that you think makes them particularly unattractive or problematic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if there's one specific thing... it's just the whole combination of it. It just makes me look so old and weathered, you know? Like I haven't taken care of myself or something. I guess I just feel like it makes me look... I don't know, worn out. Like I'm past my prime and everyone can see the toll the work has taken on me. It's just hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time where someone's compliment or reassurance about your appearance made you feel better about your face and hands for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my wife tries, like I mentioned before. She'll tell me I look fine, or that the spots and lines aren't a big deal. But... it doesn't really change anything for me. \n\nI might appreciate her trying to be nice, but as soon as I look down at my hands again or catch my face in the mirror, I'm right back to seeing it all. It just doesn't sink in. So, no, I wouldn't say reassurance really helps or makes me feel better about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could change over time, or do you feel they will always be a problem for you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... I'd like to think it could get better, maybe if I stopped working out in the sun so much, or... I don't know. But right now, it feels like it's just going to keep getting worse. The older I get, the more the skin sags and the more spots show up. So, to be honest, I kind of feel like this is just how it's going to be from here on out. It's hard to imagine not being bothered by it.\n\nDoctor: If you were to receive a compliment from someone you really respect about your appearance, do you think it would change how you feel about your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I really don't think it would. I mean... even if it was someone I had a lot of respect for, I'd probably just think they were being polite. Or, you know, maybe I'd feel okay for a minute or two, but as soon as I saw my hands again, or caught my reflection... I'd just see the exact same sagging skin and spots all over again. \n\nWhat someone else says doesn't change what's actually right there in front of me. So, no, I don't think a compliment would really fix how I see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could lessen if you were able to change your appearance in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about changing my appearance, like with surgery or anything like that. I guess I just don't know if it would actually help, or if I'd just find something else wrong with how it turned out. So, I don't really have a good answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular aspect of your face or hands that you think is the most unattractive or bothersome to you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's really the whole rough and worn look of them. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin. It's... it's just what brought me in here in the first place. I can't get past how bad it looks. Whenever I have to go into town, I feel like every banker or county clerk is just staring right at those cracks and spots. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't see it that way. I just see how ruined my skin looks.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one specific flaw in your appearance that you are most concerned about?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's just one specific thing, no. Like I was saying, it's the whole combination... the sagging, the spots, the cracks on my hands and face. It's not like there's one single spot or one deep wrinkle that bothers me more than the rest. It's just the overall way my skin looks entirely. It all just looks worn out and ruined to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your appearance if someone were able to completely fix the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, if it were actually possible to just wipe all of that away completely... I'd like to think I wouldn't worry about it anymore. But, well, I'm just not sure. It's hard to guess how I'd feel, since I know it can't just be erased like that. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be satisfied with your appearance if the specific issues with your face and hands, like the cracks and spots, were completely resolved?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a minute ago... I just can't say for sure. It all just looks worn out and ruined to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your appearance if someone were able to completely fix the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, if it were actually possible to just wipe all of that away completely... I'd like to think I wouldn't worry about it anymore. But, well, I'm just not sure. It's hard to guess how I'd feel, since I know it can't just be erased like that. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be satisfied with your appearance if the specific issues with your face and hands, like the cracks and spots, were completely resolved?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a minute ago... I really don't know. I mean, I'd hope I would be satisfied. If all those cracks and spots were just gone, maybe I could finally stop thinking about it every time I catch my reflection. But... I just can't say for sure. Since it's not something that can actually happen, I just don't have a definite answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see the flaws in your face and hands as clearly as you can?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look, and I feel like everybody else sees it just as clear. Like, if I have to go into town and talk to a banker or some county person, I swear they're just staring right at the cracks and spots and the sagging. Now, my wife... she says they aren't. She tells me I just look like a normal man who's worked outdoors his whole life. But I just don't see it that way. I feel like it's plain as day to anyone looking at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance would disappear if the cracks on your hands were completely gone?\n\nPatient: Well, doc, I feel like we're kind of going in circles here. Like I just said a minute ago, I really don't know. Even if it was just the cracks on my hands getting fixed... I'd like to think the worry would go away, but I just can't say for sure. Since it's not something that can actually happen, I really don't have a different answer for you on that. I'm just not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, hello doctor. I guess what brings me in today is... I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. I know I've been doing farm work for a long time, but the cracks, the spots, the sagging skin—it's just really getting to me. \n\nWhenever I have to go into town and deal with a banker or some county person, I feel like they're just staring right at it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a man who's worked outdoors, but... I don't know. I just can't stop focusing on it, and it's making things hard.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the appearance of your face and hands most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really do. Like I was just saying, I just can't seem to get past it. It's on my mind pretty much all day. \n\nI'll just be going about my day, and I keep focusing on the cracks and the spots, or thinking about how my face sags. And like I mentioned, if I have to go into town, it's all I can focus on. I just keep picturing those bankers or county folks staring at me, judging how worn out I look. My wife keeps telling me I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a farmer, but... yeah, it's definitely something I'm thinking about most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about the appearance of your face and hands interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean... I've actually missed calls with the bankers, and I've put off some important farm decisions lately. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to actually sit down in a room with people. \n\nAnd on top of the regular farm work, we have a retreat and do some craft sales, and I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't let people see me like that. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands that you're concerned about, or is it your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying before, it's really just those specific things. I mean, my overall appearance... I don't really worry about the rest of my body. \n\nIt's just... I really can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin—that's exactly what I feel like every banker or county person is staring at when I talk to them. Even though my wife keeps telling me I just look like a normal guy who's worked outdoors his whole life, those specific areas are what I'm completely hung up on. So, yeah, it's not everything, just the face and the hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, to me, they really do. When I look in the mirror or look down at my hands, I see exactly how bad they are. \n\nI know my wife keeps saying I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a regular guy who works outside, so... I don't know, maybe other people don't see it quite as harsh as I do. But yeah, when I'm looking at myself, I absolutely think they look as bad as I feel they do. It's hard to believe anything else when it's right there in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands don't look as bad as you think they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess there's a possibility. I mean, my wife is pretty honest with me, and she swears I'm blowing it out of proportion. So maybe logically I know that's possible. But it sure doesn't feel that way when I look at them. I just can't shake the feeling that they look awful, even if part of me knows I might be wrong.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the cracks, spots, or sagging skin that you think makes them particularly unattractive or problematic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if there's one specific thing... it's just the whole combination of it. It just makes me look so old and weathered, you know? Like I haven't taken care of myself or something. I guess I just feel like it makes me look... I don't know, worn out. Like I'm past my prime and everyone can see the toll the work has taken on me. It's just hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time where someone's compliment or reassurance about your appearance made you feel better about your face and hands for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my wife tries, like I mentioned before. She'll tell me I look fine, or that the spots and lines aren't a big deal. But... it doesn't really change anything for me. \n\nI might appreciate her trying to be nice, but as soon as I look down at my hands again or catch my face in the mirror, I'm right back to seeing it all. It just doesn't sink in. So, no, I wouldn't say reassurance really helps or makes me feel better about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could change over time, or do you feel they will always be a problem for you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... I'd like to think it could get better, maybe if I stopped working out in the sun so much, or... I don't know. But right now, it feels like it's just going to keep getting worse. The older I get, the more the skin sags and the more spots show up. So, to be honest, I kind of feel like this is just how it's going to be from here on out. It's hard to imagine not being bothered by it.\n\nDoctor: If you were to receive a compliment from someone you really respect about your appearance, do you think it would change how you feel about your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I really don't think it would. I mean... even if it was someone I had a lot of respect for, I'd probably just think they were being polite. Or, you know, maybe I'd feel okay for a minute or two, but as soon as I saw my hands again, or caught my reflection... I'd just see the exact same sagging skin and spots all over again. \n\nWhat someone else says doesn't change what's actually right there in front of me. So, no, I don't think a compliment would really fix how I see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could lessen if you were able to change your appearance in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about changing my appearance, like with surgery or anything like that. I guess I just don't know if it would actually help, or if I'd just find something else wrong with how it turned out. So, I don't really have a good answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular aspect of your face or hands that you think is the most unattractive or bothersome to you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's really the whole rough and worn look of them. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin. It's... it's just what brought me in here in the first place. I can't get past how bad it looks. Whenever I have to go into town, I feel like every banker or county clerk is just staring right at those cracks and spots. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't see it that way. I just see how ruined my skin looks.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one specific flaw in your appearance that you are most concerned about?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's just one specific thing, no. Like I was saying, it's the whole combination... the sagging, the spots, the cracks on my hands and face. It's not like there's one single spot or one deep wrinkle that bothers me more than the rest. It's just the overall way my skin looks entirely. It all just looks worn out and ruined to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your appearance if someone were able to completely fix the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, if it were actually possible to just wipe all of that away completely... I'd like to think I wouldn't worry about it anymore. But, well, I'm just not sure. It's hard to guess how I'd feel, since I know it can't just be erased like that. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be satisfied with your appearance if the specific issues with your face and hands, like the cracks and spots, were completely resolved?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a minute ago... Or, you know, maybe I'd feel okay for a minute or two, but as soon as I saw my hands again, or caught my reflection... I'd just see the exact same sagging skin and spots all over again. \n\nWhat someone else says doesn't change what's actually right there in front of me. So, no, I don't think a compliment would really fix how I see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could lessen if you were able to change your appearance in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about changing my appearance, like with surgery or anything like that. I guess I just don't know if it would actually help, or if I'd just find something else wrong with how it turned out. So, I don't really have a good answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular aspect of your face or hands that you think is the most unattractive or bothersome to you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's really the whole rough and worn look of them. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin. It's... it's just what brought me in here in the first place. I can't get past how bad it looks. Whenever I have to go into town, I feel like every banker or county clerk is just staring right at those cracks and spots. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't see it that way. I just see how ruined my skin looks.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one specific flaw in your appearance that you are most concerned about?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's just one specific thing, no. Like I was saying, it's the whole combination... the sagging, the spots, the cracks on my hands and face. It's not like there's one single spot or one deep wrinkle that bothers me more than the rest. It's just the overall way my skin looks entirely. It all just looks worn out and ruined to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your appearance if someone were able to completely fix the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, if it were actually possible to just wipe all of that away completely... I'd like to think I wouldn't worry about it anymore. But, well, I'm just not sure. It's hard to guess how I'd feel, since I know it can't just be erased like that. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be satisfied with your appearance if the specific issues with your face and hands, like the cracks and spots, were completely resolved?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a minute ago... I really don't know. I mean, I'd hope I would be satisfied. If all those cracks and spots were just gone, maybe I could finally stop thinking about it every time I catch my reflection. But... I just can't say for sure. Since it's not something that can actually happen, I just don't have a definite answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see the flaws in your face and hands as clearly as you can?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look, and I feel like everybody else sees it just as clear. Like, if I have to go into town and talk to a banker or some county person, I swear they're just staring right at the cracks and spots and the sagging. Now, my wife... she says they aren't. She tells me I just look like a normal man who's worked outdoors his whole life. But I just don't see it that way. I feel like it's plain as day to anyone looking at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance would disappear if the cracks on your hands were completely gone?\n\nPatient: Well, doc, I feel like we're kind of going in circles here. Like I just said a minute ago, I really don't know. Even if it was just the cracks on my hands getting fixed... I'd like to think the worry would go away, but I just can't say for sure. Since it's not something that can actually happen, I really don't have a different answer for you on that. I'm just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Is there something about your appearance that you think would need to change for you to no longer be concerned about how you look?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Honestly, I'm not sure there is any one thing. Like I said, I'd like to think fixing the spots or the cracks would do it, but... I don't know if that's true. I just don't have a good answer for you on what it would take to make me stop worrying about it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. My wife keeps telling me I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a farmer, but... yeah, it's definitely something I'm thinking about most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about the appearance of your face and hands interfering with your daily activities or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. I mean... I've actually missed calls with the bankers, and I've put off some important farm decisions lately. I just couldn't get myself looking acceptable enough to actually sit down in a room with people. \n\nAnd on top of the regular farm work, we have a retreat and do some craft sales, and I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't let people see me like that. So, yeah, it's definitely getting in the way of things.\n\nDoctor: Is it specifically the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands that you're concerned about, or is it your overall appearance?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying before, it's really just those specific things. I mean, my overall appearance... I don't really worry about the rest of my body. \n\nIt's just... I really can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin—that's exactly what I feel like every banker or county person is staring at when I talk to them. Even though my wife keeps telling me I just look like a normal guy who's worked outdoors his whole life, those specific areas are what I'm completely hung up on. So, yeah, it's not everything, just the face and the hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, to me, they really do. When I look in the mirror or look down at my hands, I see exactly how bad they are. \n\nI know my wife keeps saying I'm making a big deal out of nothing and that I just look like a regular guy who works outside, so... I don't know, maybe other people don't see it quite as harsh as I do. But yeah, when I'm looking at myself, I absolutely think they look as bad as I feel they do. It's hard to believe anything else when it's right there in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands don't look as bad as you think they do?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess there's a possibility. I mean, my wife is pretty honest with me, and she swears I'm blowing it out of proportion. So maybe logically I know that's possible. But it sure doesn't feel that way when I look at them. I just can't shake the feeling that they look awful, even if part of me knows I might be wrong.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the cracks, spots, or sagging skin that you think makes them particularly unattractive or problematic?\n\nPatient: I don't know if there's one specific thing... it's just the whole combination of it. It just makes me look so old and weathered, you know? Like I haven't taken care of myself or something. I guess I just feel like it makes me look... I don't know, worn out. Like I'm past my prime and everyone can see the toll the work has taken on me. It's just hard to look at.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time where someone's compliment or reassurance about your appearance made you feel better about your face and hands for a while?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my wife tries, like I mentioned before. She'll tell me I look fine, or that the spots and lines aren't a big deal. But... it doesn't really change anything for me. \n\nI might appreciate her trying to be nice, but as soon as I look down at my hands again or catch my face in the mirror, I'm right back to seeing it all. It just doesn't sink in. So, no, I wouldn't say reassurance really helps or makes me feel better about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could change over time, or do you feel they will always be a problem for you?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... I'd like to think it could get better, maybe if I stopped working out in the sun so much, or... I don't know. But right now, it feels like it's just going to keep getting worse. The older I get, the more the skin sags and the more spots show up. So, to be honest, I kind of feel like this is just how it's going to be from here on out. It's hard to imagine not being bothered by it.\n\nDoctor: If you were to receive a compliment from someone you really respect about your appearance, do you think it would change how you feel about your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I really don't think it would. I mean... even if it was someone I had a lot of respect for, I'd probably just think they were being polite. Or, you know, maybe I'd feel okay for a minute or two, but as soon as I saw my hands again, or caught my reflection... I'd just see the exact same sagging skin and spots all over again. \n\nWhat someone else says doesn't change what's actually right there in front of me. So, no, I don't think a compliment would really fix how I see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands could lessen if you were able to change your appearance in some way?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about changing my appearance, like with surgery or anything like that. I guess I just don't know if it would actually help, or if I'd just find something else wrong with how it turned out. So, I don't really have a good answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Is there one particular aspect of your face or hands that you think is the most unattractive or bothersome to you?\n\nPatient: Well, it's really the whole rough and worn look of them. The cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin. It's... it's just what brought me in here in the first place. I can't get past how bad it looks. Whenever I have to go into town, I feel like every banker or county clerk is just staring right at those cracks and spots. My wife tells me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, but... I don't see it that way. I just see how ruined my skin looks.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is one specific flaw in your appearance that you are most concerned about?\n\nPatient: I don't think it's just one specific thing, no. Like I was saying, it's the whole combination... the sagging, the spots, the cracks on my hands and face. It's not like there's one single spot or one deep wrinkle that bothers me more than the rest. It's just the overall way my skin looks entirely. It all just looks worn out and ruined to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be concerned about your appearance if someone were able to completely fix the cracks, spots, and sagging skin on your face and hands?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean, if it were actually possible to just wipe all of that away completely... I'd like to think I wouldn't worry about it anymore. But, well, I'm just not sure. It's hard to guess how I'd feel, since I know it can't just be erased like that. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be satisfied with your appearance if the specific issues with your face and hands, like the cracks and spots, were completely resolved?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was just saying a minute ago... I really don't know. I mean, I'd hope I would be satisfied. If all those cracks and spots were just gone, maybe I could finally stop thinking about it every time I catch my reflection. But... I just can't say for sure. Since it's not something that can actually happen, I just don't have a definite answer for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see the flaws in your face and hands as clearly as you can?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I just can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look, and I feel like everybody else sees it just as clear. Like, if I have to go into town and talk to a banker or some county person, I swear they're just staring right at the cracks and spots and the sagging. Now, my wife... she says they aren't. She tells me I just look like a normal man who's worked outdoors his whole life. But I just don't see it that way. I feel like it's plain as day to anyone looking at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance would disappear if the cracks on your hands were completely gone?\n\nPatient: Well, doc, I feel like we're kind of going in circles here. Like I just said a minute ago, I really don't know. Even if it was just the cracks on my hands getting fixed... I'd like to think the worry would go away, but I just can't say for sure. Since it's not something that can actually happen, I really don't have a different answer for you on that. I'm just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Is there something about your appearance that you think would need to change for you to no longer be concerned about how you look?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Honestly, I'm not sure there is any one thing. Like I said, I'd like to think fixing the spots or the cracks would do it, but... I don't know if that's true. I just don't have a good answer for you on what it would take to make me stop worrying about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your face and hands would lessen if you were told by a medical professional that your appearance is within the normal range for someone of your age and occupation?\n\nPatient: I don't think that would change much for me, to be honest. My regular doctor has actually told me something like that before—that my skin just looks like a farmer's skin. But hearing it doesn't really make a difference in how I feel when I look in the mirror or when I'm out in public. I hear the words, but the worry is still there just the same."
